{"parentUuid":null,"isSidechain":true,"promptId":"77a899ff-1e86-45ed-8041-dbe3ce017ec3","agentId":"a8133cd58490ff89f","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":"READ-ONLY CLASSIFICATION AUDIT of /home/cklaus/projects/axon.\n\nABSOLUTE CONSTRAINTS — a `gate.sh --strict` run is executing in this checkout RIGHT NOW:\n- Do NOT edit/create/delete ANY file. Report as text only.\n- Do NOT run cargo (build/test/check/clippy/run), scripts/, or any binary. A\n  rebuild would corrupt a running gate.\n- Read-only: rg/grep, find, sed -n, cat, head, tail, git log/show.\n\nTHE TASK. `crates/axon-core/src/env_registry.rs` has `ALL_ENV_VARS` — roughly 50\n`AXON_*` controls, gated in both directions against `AXON_REFERENCE.md`.\nClassify EVERY one of them by which execution engine actually honours it.\n\nThis exists because three silent divergences were just PROVEN BY EXECUTION:\n\n* `AXON_ALLOWED_EFFECTS` — interpreter refused a `println` under a `Pure`\n  ceiling (exit 8); a natively built binary printed and exited 0.\n* `AXON_REPLAY` — interpreter replay created no file; the native binary printed\n  the replayed output AND performed the write for real, exit 0.\n* `AXON_AUDIT_LEDGER` — interpreter wrote 2 entries for one `write_file`; the\n  native binary did not create the ledger file at all.\n* `AXON_SEED` — native ignored it completely: seeds 1, 42 and 999 all produced\n  289383, because codegen lowers `random_i64` to a bare C `rand()` and `srand`\n  was called nowhere.\n\nAll four are now fixed (native enforces the seed; the other three are refused).\nThe triage signal that found them: **`axon-rt` reads 3 of the ~50 variables and\n`crates/axon-core/src/codegen/` reads 1.** That is not proof the other 46 are\nbroken — it is a reason to classify them rather than inspect ad hoc.\n\nFOR EVERY variable in `ALL_ENV_VARS`, produce a row:\n\n  name | category | intended engines | actual engines | observable behaviour | existing test | parity status\n\n**category** — one of:\n  authorization/effect-ceiling · replay/record/audit · determinism (RNG/clock) ·\n  filesystem/network/process · approval/attestation · resource-budget ·\n  guest-boot-policy · developer-convenience · diagnostics\n\n**intended engines** — what the docs (CLAUDE.md, AXON_REFERENCE.md, the registry\ndescription) say or imply. Quote if a doc states it.\n\n**actual engines** — where a READ of the variable exists, cited file:line. Check\nthese separately and do not infer one from another:\n  interpreter  `crates/axon-core/src/interp*`\n  native       `crates/axon-core/src/codegen/*` AND `crates/axon-rt/*`\n  wasm         `crates/axon-wasm/*`\n  guest/OS     `crates/axon-os/*`, `crates/axon-guest-init/*`, `crates/axon-vm/*`\n\n**parity status** — one of:\n  CONSISTENT (all intended engines read it)\n  SILENT-DIVERGENCE (an intended engine does not read it and does not refuse) ← the finding that matters\n  REFUSED (an engine explicitly refuses rather than ignoring)\n  N-A (meaningful for one engine only, and that is correct)\n  UNKNOWN (cannot be settled by reading; say what would settle it)\n\n**existing test** — name the test or harness that would catch a regression, or\nwrite NONE. A variable whose only \"coverage\" is the registry drift gate has NONE,\nbecause that gate checks the variable is READ SOMEWHERE, not that it is honoured\nby the right engines.\n\nPRIORITISE, in this order — these are where silent native divergence would\naffect trust:\n  1. authorization/effect-ceiling\n  2. replay/record/audit\n  3. determinism (RNG/clock)\n  4. filesystem/network/process\n  5. approval/attestation\n  6. resource-budget\n  7. anything read by guest boot policy (`crates/axon-guest-init`)\n\nEverything in categories 1-7 must get a full row. Developer-convenience and\ndiagnostics variables can be grouped with a one-line justification for why they\ncarry no trust consequence — but say which ones you put there and why, because\nthat classification is itself a claim.\n\nDELIVER: the table, then a ranked list of every SILENT-DIVERGENCE found, each\nwith the concrete experiment that would confirm it by execution (the shape:\n\"same program, same variable, run under engine A and engine B, observe X\"). Do\nNOT run them.\n\nAn empty SILENT-DIVERGENCE list is a valid and welcome result — but say how you\nestablished it."},"uuid":"332e1f25-ac66-408b-bd3d-210e4d227d7c","timestamp":"2026-09-20T19:55:20.266Z","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/home/cklaus/projects/axon","sessionId":"b08bcf98-5d80-4faf-ab6a-34e0772b5071","version":"2.1.223","gitBranch":"main","slug":"valiant-dancing-bird"}

{"parentUuid":"889fcfa1-fa19-45bc-9474-128127816def","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"a8133cd58490ff89f","attachment":{"type":"skill_listing","content":"- blind-tui-loop: Iteratively improve a TUI against a competitive reference set using blind evaluation — the orchestrator coordinates but never judges UI quality. Beat map → rubric → benchmark → prioritised queue → 3 independent builders → render real states → blind tournament where the baseline always competes. Use when the user wants a terminal UI made category-leading, or invokes /blind-tui-loop.\n- build-loop: Autonomously build a spec end-to-end — adversarial review, resolve every open question, author a DAG-based build harness, then execute unattended to a compound stop condition (full-suite green, no unaddressed spec items, real smoke test passing). Use when the user wants a spec/plan/hardening-doc/feature-list built out with minimal hand-holding, or explicitly invokes /build-loop.\n- startup-pipeline: Run the AI startup pipeline — brainstorm from a theme (or take one defined idea), select top ideas via judge panel, define each startup (naming, ICPs, roadmap, competitors, team), research it, and produce an MVP PRD + spec. Use when the user invokes /startup-pipeline or asks to develop a startup theme/idea end-to-end.\n- wikiresearch: Systematically research the strongest peer, adjacent, and best-in-class open-source repositories relative to a target repository, extract reusable architecture/product/algorithm knowledge, benchmark capabilities against the target with evidence, and turn the highest-value findings into a durable research wiki, core-vs-extension decisions, and evidence-backed specs. Use for comparative/competitive ecosystem research on a repo, \"what are the best systems in this category doing\", plugin-vs-core architecture calls backed by research, or explicit /wikiresearch invocation. Not for a quick one-off \"how does X compare to Y\" answer — see Scope calibration below.\n- optimize-skill: Optimize a Claude Code skill using SkillOpt methodology — mines session transcripts, reflects on failures/successes, proposes bounded edits.\n- optimize-skill-fast: Fan out N parallel skill optimization trials, score each variant, apply the best — much faster than sequential nightly cycles.\n- trainloop-analyze: Deep cross-pass analysis of trainloop+megabrain — research only, no code changes\n- trainloop-improve: Ship ONE small concrete improvement to trainloop or megabrain — picks, ships, stops\n- dataviz: Use this skill whenever you are about to create ANY chart, graph, plot, dashboard, or data visualization, in ANY output medium — an HTML or React artifact, inline SVG, plotting code in any library (matplotlib, plotly, d3, Recharts, …), an image/PNG you will render and upload, or a chart shared into Slack. Read it BEFORE writing the first line of chart code, choosing chart colors, building a stat tile / meter / KPI row, or laying out a dashboard. Produces visualizations that read as one system — elegant, accessible, consistent in light and dark — using a brand-neutral placeholder palette you swap for your own. Teaches a design-system-agnostic method: a form heuristic, a color formula with a runnable validator, mark specs, and interaction rules. A validated default palette is documented in `references/palette.md` — swap that file's values for your brand's. Triggers on: \"chart\", \"graph\", \"plot\", \"data viz\", \"visualization\", \"dashboard\", \"analytics\", \"visualize data\", \"categorical colors\", \"sequential / diverging palette\", \"stat tile\", \"sparkline\", \"heatmap\", \"legend\", \"axis\", \"tooltip\", \"chart colors\", \"color by series\".\n- artifact-design: Design guidance and fundamentals for Artifacts.\n- artifact-diagramming: Diagramming know-how for Artifacts — when a picture earns its place, how to draw one that shows the real mechanism, and the inline-SVG mechanics that keep it legible in both themes.\n- artifact-capabilities: Runtime capabilities a published Artifact page can be granted — behavior static HTML cannot provide on its own, such as the page reading live or connected data, keeping state shared across viewers, or updating and republishing itself. Serves this user's live capability roster and the typed call definitions. Load it whenever the user asks for an artifact needing any such runtime behavior.\n- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- code-review: Review the current diff, or a PR number/branch/path target, for correctness bugs and reuse/simplification/efficiency cleanups at the given effort level (low/medium: fewer, high-confidence findings; high→max: broader coverage, may include uncertain findings; ultra: deep multi-agent review in the cloud); with no level given, it reuses the level you typed last. Pass --comment to post findings as inline PR comments, or --fix to apply the findings to the working tree after the review.\n- simplify: Review the changed code for reuse, simplification, efficiency, and altitude cleanups, then apply the fixes. Quality only — it does not hunt for bugs; use /code-review for that.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled cloud agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring cloud agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Reference for the Claude API / Anthropic SDK — model ids, pricing, params, streaming, tool use, MCP, agents, caching, token counting, model migration.\nTRIGGER — read BEFORE opening the target file; don't skip because it \"looks like a one-liner\" — whenever: the prompt names Claude/Anthropic in any form (Claude, Anthropic, Fable, Opus, Sonnet, Haiku, `anthropic`, `@anthropic-ai`, `claude-*`, `us.anthropic.*`, `[1m]`); the user asks about an LLM (pricing/model choice/limits/caching) — never answer from memory; OR the task is LLM-shaped with provider unstated (agent/MCP/tool-definition/multi-agent/RAG/LLM-judge/computer-use; generate/summarize/extract/classify/rewrite/converse over NL; debugging refusals/cutoffs/streaming/tool-calls/tokens).\nSKIP only when another provider is being worked on (overrides all triggers): OpenAI/GPT/Gemini/Llama/Mistral/Cohere/Ollama named in the query; OR `grep -rE 'openai|langchain_openai|google.generativeai|genai|mistralai|cohere|ollama'` over the project hits (run this grep FIRST if no provider named — don't Read the file).\n- run: Launch and drive this project's app to see a change working. Use when asked to run, start, or screenshot the app, or to confirm a change works in the real app (not just tests). First looks for a project skill that already covers launching the app; otherwise falls back to built-in patterns per project type (CLI, server, TUI, Electron, browser-driven, library).\n- init: Initialize a new CLAUDE.md file with codebase documentation\n- security-review: Complete a security review of the pending changes on the current branch","skillCount":23,"isInitial":true,"names":["blind-tui-loop","build-loop","startup-pipeline","wikiresearch","optimize-skill","optimize-skill-fast","trainloop-analyze","trainloop-improve","dataviz","artifact-design","artifact-diagramming","artifact-capabilities","update-config","keybindings-help","code-review","simplify","fewer-permission-prompts","loop","schedule","claude-api","run","init","security-review"]},"type":"attachment","uuid":"9f39f63f-a598-43b7-a658-c409ee4e7cfd","timestamp":"2026-09-20T19:55:20.288Z","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/home/cklaus/projects/axon","sessionId":"b08bcf98-5d80-4faf-ab6a-34e0772b5071","version":"2.1.223","gitBranch":"main","slug":"valiant-dancing-bird"}

{"parentUuid":"240b3da5-54df-4d1d-8d0a-0c33be5c11e0","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"77a899ff-1e86-45ed-8041-dbe3ce017ec3","agentId":"a8133cd58490ff89f","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01J1wJREqoX8nQA7bxiEZmkw","type":"tool_result","content":"211 /home/cklaus/projects/axon/crates/axon-core/src/env_registry.rs\n//! Every `AXON_*` environment variable the shipped code reads, with what it does.\n//!\n//! WHY THIS EXISTS.\n//!\n//! The meanings lived only in `CLAUDE.md`'s table, which documents 21 of 47 and\n//! says so. That is the right call for a curated card — but it left no tool able\n//! to enumerate them, so `axon reference` could describe verbs, builtins and\n//! diagnostic codes exhaustively and say nothing about the knobs that change how\n//! a run BEHAVES. A var that silently does nothing (or silently does something)\n//! is the worst case: `AXON_ALLOWED_EFFECTS` and `AXON_BUDGET_TOKENS` were both\n//! documented as enforced while being read by nothing at all, and only a\n//! behavioural diff found it.\n//!\n//! Scope is the SHIPPED source (`crates/*/src`), not test harnesses. A variable\n//! only a test reads is not part of the language, and listing it would imply a\n//! supported knob.\n\n/// `(name, description)` for every `AXON_*` var read by shipped code.\n///\n/// Gated in both directions by the tests below: a var read without a row here\n/// fails, and a row here for a var nothing reads fails too.\npub const ALL_ENV_VARS: &[(&str, &str)] = &[\n    // ── Determinism & execution ──────────────────────────────────────────\n    (\"AXON_SEED\", \"seed the RNG (u64) so `random_*` runs reproduce\"),\n    (\"AXON_MAX_DEPTH\", \"recursion-depth ceiling (default 6000, clamped to 1,000,000); the interpreter thread stack scales with it\"),\n    (\"AXON_CLOCK\", \"deterministic virtual clock `<start_ms>[:<tick_ms>]`; `sleep_ms` advances it without really sleeping\"),\n    (\"AXON_PATH\", \"colon-separated module search path for `mod`/`use` imports\"),\n    (\"AXON_STRICT\", \"promote advisory hazard diagnostics to errors (today E0302, an unused Result); `axon deploy` sets it itself\"),\n    // ── Record / replay ──────────────────────────────────────────────────\n    (\"AXON_RECORD\", \"path to write a host journal: every call through the AxonHost seam, performed for real and appended with its outcome. As sensitive as the run it records\"),\n    (\"AXON_REPLAY\", \"serve a run from a host journal instead of the world; nothing is performed, and any miss is a divergence (exit 11). Mutually exclusive with AXON_RECORD\"),\n    (\"AXON_AI_REPLAY\", \"path to an LLM-call replay cache; memoizes `ai_complete` by (prompt, model) so an AI run reproduces with no live call\"),\n    // ── AI routing & policy ──────────────────────────────────────────────\n    (\"AXON_AI_MOCK\", \"use deterministic stub AI responses instead of live calls (the real per-token cost is still metered)\"),\n    (\"AXON_CORTEX_GENERATOR_TIMEOUT_MS\", \"how long `cortex repair --generator cmd:PATH` waits for the operator's program to answer (default 120s). Exists so the deadline can be TESTED: a suite cannot wait two minutes, and an untested deadline is the kind of check that turns out never to fire. A malformed or zero value is ignored in favour of the default rather than disabling every generator\"),\n    (\"AXON_AI_PROVIDER\", \"live-AI codec: `anthropic` or `openai`\"),\n    (\"AXON_AI_BASE_URL\", \"gateway URL for live AI calls\"),\n    (\"AXON_AI_API_KEY\", \"API key for live AI calls\"),\n    (\"AXON_AI_MODEL_CHEAP\", \"override the model `@[ai(tier: cheap)]` resolves to\"),\n    (\"AXON_AI_MODEL_BALANCED\", \"override the model `@[ai(tier: balanced)]` resolves to\"),\n    (\"AXON_AI_MODEL_STRONG\", \"override the model `@[ai(tier: strong)]` resolves to\"),\n    (\"AXON_BUDGET_TOKENS\", \"run-wide AI token cap; the call that would exceed it halts with E1303 (exit 5) BEFORE dispatch. A malformed value fails closed to 0\"),\n    (\"AXON_DOTENV\", \"path to a `.env` file to load for AI configuration\"),\n    (\"AXON_DOTENV_WALK\", \"walk parent directories looking for a `.env` file\"),\n    (\"AXON_INTENT_GEN\", \"let `axon intent compile` fill TODO stubs via a live model (needs `--features asi-runtime`)\"),\n    // ── Capabilities, principals, audit ──────────────────────────────────\n    (\"AXON_ALLOWED_EFFECTS\", \"ambient effect ceiling for the whole run; a true ceiling that an inner sandbox may narrow but never widen. EMPTY means deny every effect and is not the same as unset. Interpreter-only\"),\n    (\"AXON_PRINCIPAL\", \"the principal a run executes as — audit ATTRIBUTION only; it grants and withholds nothing\"),\n    (\"AXON_GUEST_ALLOW_NO_POLICY\", \"axon-guest-init: start the guest even though no MMDS capability policy could be loaded (no effect ceiling, no token cap, no seccomp). Development only — without it an unreadable policy REFUSES to start the guest, because an absent policy is not a permissive one\"),\n    (\"AXON_REQUIRE_CERTS\", \"fail closed on the R23 solver-free kernel-mint certificate check instead of the default silent pass\"),\n    (\"AXON_ATTEST_KEY\", \"operator-provisioned attestation key (hex, >=16 bytes). When set, axon-vm signs AND verifies the attestation report under it, so a report signed by anyone else fails. Unset falls back to an ephemeral per-process key, where signer and verifier are the same process — real integrity over the measurement, but attesting nothing to a third party\"),\n    (\"AXON_AUDIT_LEDGER\", \"path to the R28 capability audit ledger\"),\n    (\"AXON_AUDIT_DETERMINISTIC\", \"use a counter instead of a clock for ledger timestamps, so audit output is reproducible in tests\"),\n    (\"AXON_KILL_FILE\", \"kill file the axon-os supervisor polls; its EXISTENCE trips nothing — the job stops only once its CONTENT reads `{\\\"latch\\\":\\\"tripped\\\"}`\"),\n    // ── Goal search ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n    (\"AXON_GOAL_CONTINUE\", \"resume a `goal` search from the best prior input in the provenance log (set automatically by `axon goal --iterate`)\"),\n    // ── Session (R44) ────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n    (\"AXON_DUMP_BINDINGS\", \"write a cell's final bindings back as Axon source literals — the session substrate, also usable by an external driver\"),\n    (\"AXON_DUMP_SHAPES\", \"write a `name: shape` inventory of every binding, including ones that could not be persisted\"),\n    // ── SMT proving ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n    (\"AXON_PROOF_TIMEOUT_MS\", \"per-obligation SMT solver timeout; 0 selects the Z3-free runtime-check fallback\"),\n    (\"AXON_PROOF_DEPTH\", \"SMT unrolling/search depth bound\"),\n    // ── Substrates: VM, OS, wasm, native ─────────────────────────────────\n    (\"AXON_HOST_SOCKET\", \"guest-kernel hypercall bridge socket; takes priority over AXON_VM_VSOCK_PORT for `host_await`\"),\n    (\"AXON_VM_VSOCK_PORT\", \"vsock port for `host_await` inside an axon-vm microVM (set by the launcher)\"),\n    (\"AXON_VM_ALLOWED_EFFECTS\", \"effect ceiling delivered to a guest via the VM's MMDS policy\"),\n    (\"AXON_VM_TIMEOUT_SECS\", \"wall-clock bound on a microVM run\"),\n    (\"AXON_VM_SOCKET_TIMEOUT_SECS\", \"bound on waiting for the VM's control socket\"),\n    (\"AXON_VM_DEBUG\", \"verbose microVM launch diagnostics\"),\n    (\"AXON_VM_QUIET\", \"suppress microVM launch chatter\"),\n    (\"AXON_CONFIG_DIR\", \"override the config directory the VM reads its Principal registry from\"),\n    (\"AXON_CI_NO_KVM\", \"force mock mode where /dev/kvm is unavailable (CI)\"),\n    (\"AXON_OS_TIMEOUT_MS\", \"axon-os bound on a supervised job (`run_bounded`)\"),\n    (\"AXON_WASM_RT\", \"path to the wasm runtime used to execute a built wasm artifact\"),\n    (\"AXON_ANDROID_API\", \"Android API level for the NDK cross-link\"),\n    (\"AXON_NATIVE_TRACE\", \"trace native-FFI module calls (gfx mock)\"),\n    (\"AXON_BIN\", \"path to the `axon` binary an out-of-process synthesizer should invoke\"),\n    (\"AXON_INTENT_TIMEOUT_MS\", \"bound on the `axon intent` subprocess synthesizer\"),\n    // ── Test fixtures that shipped code reads ────────────────────────────\n    (\"AXON_TEST_DOTENV_VAR\", \"(test fixture) name of a variable the .env loader test expects to find\"),\n    (\"AXON_TEST_DOTENV_NEW\", \"(test fixture) asserts the As sensitive as the run it records\"),\n    (\"AXON_REPLAY\", \"serve a run from a host journal instead of the world; nothing is performed, and any miss is a divergence (exit 11). Exists so the deadline can be TESTED: a suite cannot wait two minutes, and an untested deadline is the kind of check that turns out never to fire. A malformed or zero value is ignored in favour of the default rather than disabling every generator\"),\n    (\"AXON_AI_PROVIDER\", \"live-AI codec: `anthropic` or `openai`\"),\n    (\"AXON_AI_BASE_URL\", \"gateway URL for live AI calls\"),\n    (\"AXON_AI_API_KEY\", \"API key for live AI calls\"),\n    (\"AXON_AI_MODEL_CHEAP\", \"override the model `@[ai(tier: cheap)]` resolves to\"),\n    (\"AXON_AI_MODEL_BALANCED\", \"override the model `@[ai(tier: balanced)]` resolves to\"),\n    (\"AXON_AI_MODEL_STRONG\", \"override the model `@[ai(tier: strong)]` resolves to\"),\n    (\"AXON_BUDGET_TOKENS\", \"run-wide AI token cap; the call that would exceed it halts with E1303 (exit 5) BEFORE dispatch. {"parentUuid":"e7785407-dd31-4757-acd3-4ca8cde96049","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"77a899ff-1e86-45ed-8041-dbe3ce017ec3","agentId":"a8133cd58490ff89f","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01XNyui7Sz8N9SvXw5HW3tVN","type":"tool_result","content":"    }\n}\n\n// ── Phase 4: Time builtins ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n\n/// Suspend the current thread for at least `ms` milliseconds.\n#[no_mangle]\npub extern \"C\" fn __axon_sleep_ms(ms: i64) {\n    // Under a virtual clock, advance the timeline rather than blocking — the\n    // program still observes `ms` elapsed. Matches the interpreter.\n    if vclock::advance(ms) {\n        return;\n    }\n    if ms > 0 {\n        std::thread::sleep(std::time::Duration::from_millis(ms as u64));\n    }\n}\n\n/// Return the current wall-clock time as milliseconds since the Unix epoch.\n#[no_mangle]\npub extern \"C\" fn __axon_now_ms() -> i64 {\n    // Honour the virtual clock so a native binary replays like the interpreter\n    // does (I-2). This duplicates `axon-core`'s `clock.rs` because axon-core does\n    // not depend on axon-rt; `scripts/clock_parity.sh` is what keeps the two\n    // honest. Precedent: native silently ignored AXON_AI_MOCK until that was\n    // found and fixed the same way.\n    if let Some(t) = vclock::now_ms() {\n        return t;\n    }\n    use std::time::{SystemTime, UNIX_EPOCH};\n    SystemTime::now()\n        .duration_since(UNIX_EPOCH)\n        .map(|d| d.as_millis() as i64)\n        .unwrap_or(0)\n}\n\n/// Refuse to run a native binary under an INTERPRETER-ONLY control.\n///\n/// Neither variable is read anywhere in `codegen/` or in this crate's host\n/// paths, so a native binary silently ignores both. Measured on a program whose\n/// only effect is one `write_file`:\n///\n/// ```text\n/// interp  AXON_REPLAY=j axon run w.ax  -> \"done\", exit 0, file NOT created\n/// native  AXON_REPLAY=j ./wbin         -> \"done\", exit 0, file CREATED\n/// ```\n///\n/// The native run looks exactly like a successful replay and performs the\n/// effect anyway. `scripts/replay_host_gate.sh` states the stake in its own\n/// header: \"a replay that quietly consults live state produces an\n/// authoritative-looking transcript of a run that never happened, which is\n/// strictly worse for an auditor than having no replay at all.\"\n///\n/// `AXON_RECORD` is refused for the mirror reason: a native run would write NO\n/// journal, and an empty journal is indistinguishable from a run that touched\n/// nothing.\n///\n/// REFUSAL, not implementation. Native replay would mean routing every host\n/// call through a journal in `axon-rt`, which is a substantial piece of work;\n/// refusing costs nothing and converts a silent wrong answer into an explicit\n/// one. This is a RUN-time check because these variables are only ever set at\n/// run time — the build-time refusal used for `AXON_ALLOWED_EFFECTS` cannot\n/// apply. It is consistent with what this crate already does: it reads\n/// `AXON_CLOCK`, `AXON_AI_MOCK` and `AXON_MAX_DEPTH` at runtime today.\n#[no_mangle]\npub extern \"C\" fn __axon_rt_refuse_interp_only_env() {\n    // One list, not three one-offs. Each of these is honoured by the\n    // interpreter and read nowhere in `codegen/` or this crate's host paths,\n    // so a native binary silently ignores it. Adding a row here is the whole\n    // cost of covering the next one.\n    for var in [\"AXON_REPLAY\", \"AXON_RECORD\", \"AXON_AUDIT_LEDGER\"] {\n        if std::env::var_os(var).is_some() {\n            eprintln!(\n                \"axon: `{var}` is set, but this is a NATIVELY BUILT binary and \\\n                 the host journal is honoured by the interpreter only.\\n  \\\n                 Refusing to run: continuing would {}.\\n  \\\n                 Run the program with `axon run`, which implements the journal.\",\n                match var {\n                    \"AXON_REPLAY\" => {\n                        \"perform the effects for real while looking like a replay\"\n                    }\n                    \"AXON_RECORD\" => {\n                        \"record nothing, leaving an empty journal that reads as a \\\n                         run which touched nothing\"\n                    }\n                    // Measured: interp wrote 2 ledger entries for one\n                    // `write_file`; the native binary did not create the ledger\n                    // file at all.\n                    _ => {\n                        \"perform capability-bearing operations while writing NO \\\n                         audit entries — a ledger indistinguishable from a run \\\n                         that did nothing\"\n                    }\n                }\n            );\n            std::process::exit(2);\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// Seed the C RNG that native `random_i64`/`random_f64` lower to.\n///\n/// Native codegen emits a bare `rand()` and NOTHING ever called `srand`, so a\n/// natively built binary produced the SAME \"random\" value on every run, on\n/// every machine, and ignored `AXON_SEED` entirely. Measured on one program:\n///\n/// ```text\n/// interp   seed=1 -> 269761   seed=42 -> 75435    seed=999 -> 243488\n/// native   seed=1 -> 289383   seed=42 -> 289383   seed=999 -> 289383\n/// ```\n///\n/// Two defects in one: a documented control (`AXON_SEED`) silently dropped by\n/// one engine, and — worse, because it is not a parity question — randomness\n/// that is not random. `CLAUDE.md` gives `AXON_CLOCK` the caveat \"Honoured by\n/// interp AND native\"; the `AXON_SEED` row carries no engine caveat at all, so\n/// a reader has no way to know.\n///\n/// Called once from `main`'s prologue, alongside the recursion guard and the\n/// adaptive registry. When `AXON_SEED` is unset the seed comes from the clock,\n/// which restores ordinary randomness; when it is set the run is reproducible,\n/// which is what the variable is for.\n#[no_mangle]\npub extern \"C\" fn __axon_rt_seed_rng() {\n    let seed = match std::env::var(\"AXON_SEED\")\n        .ok()\n        .and_then(|v| v.trim().parse::<u64>().ok())\n    {\n        Some(s) => s,\n        None => std::time::SystemTime::now()\n            .duration_since(std::time::UNIX_EPOCH)\n            .map(|d| d.as_nanos() as u64)\n            .unwrap_or(1),\n    };\n    // SAFETY: `srand` takes an unsigned int and returns nothing.\n    unsafe { libc::srand(seed as libc::c_uint) };\n}\n\n/// Deterministic virtual clock for the NATIVE runtime — the mirror of\n/// `axon-core`'s `clock.rs`. See that module for the full rationale; the short\n/// version is that a monotonic virtual clock (not a frozen one) is what lets a\n/// run that reads the time be replayed without changing what the program\n/// computes.\n///\n/// `AXON_CLOCK=<start_ms>` or `<start_ms>:<tick_ms>`; tick defaults to 1 and may\n/// be 0 for a clock that only moves when `sleep_ms` advances it.\nmod vclock {\n    use std::sync::atomic::{AtomicBool, AtomicI64, Ordering};\n\n    static ENABLED: AtomicBool = AtomicBool::new(false);\n    static INITIALIZED: AtomicBool = AtomicBool::new(false);\n    static CURRENT: AtomicI64 = AtomicI64::new(0);\n    static TICK: AtomicI64 = AtomicI64::new(1);\n\n    fn init_from_env() {\n        if INITIALIZED.swap(true, Ordering::SeqCst) {\n            return;\n        }\n        let Ok(raw) = std::env::var(\"AXON_CLOCK\") else {\n            return;\n        };\n        let raw = raw.trim();\n        if raw.is_empty() {\n            return;\n        }\n        let (start, tick) = match raw.split_once(':') {\n            Some((s, t)) => (s.trim().parse::<i64>(), t.trim().parse::<i64>().ok()),\n            None => (raw.parse::<i64>(), None),\n        };\n        // Malformed input leaves the clock OFF rather than inventing a timeline.\n        if let Ok(start) = start {\n            CURRENT.store(start, Ordering::SeqCst);\n            TICK.store(tick.unwrap_or(1).max(0), Ordering::SeqCst);\n            ENABLED.store(true, Ordering::SeqCst);\n        }\n    }\n\n    fn enabled() -> bool {\n        init_from_env();\n        ENABLED.load(Ordering::SeqCst)\n    }\n\n    /// The virtual \"now\", advancing by `tick`. `None` → use the real clock.\n    pub fn now_ms() -> Option<i64> {\n        if !enabled() {\n            return None;\n        }\n        Some(CURRENT.fetch_add(TICK.load(Ordering::SeqCst), Ordering::SeqCst))\n    }\n\n    /// Advance by `ms`. `false` → the caller should really sleep.\n    pub fn advance(ms: i64) -> bool {\n        if !enabled() {\n            return false;\n        }\n        CURRENT.fetch_add(ms.max(0), Ordering::SeqCst);\n        true\n    }\n}\n\n// ── Phase 10: It is consistent with what this crate already does: it reads\n/// `AXON_CLOCK`, `AXON_AI_MOCK` and `AXON_MAX_DEPTH` at runtime today.\n#[no_mangle]\npub extern \"C\" fn __axon_rt_refuse_interp_only_env() {\n    // One list, not three one-offs. Each of these is honoured by the\n    // interpreter and read nowhere in `codegen/` or this crate's host paths,\n    // so a native binary silently ignores it. Adding a row here is the whole\n    // cost of covering the next one.\n    for var in [\"AXON_REPLAY\", \"AXON_RECORD\", \"AXON_AUDIT_LEDGER\"] {\n        if std::env::var_os(var).is_some() {\n            eprintln!(\n                \"axon: `{var}` is set, but this is a NATIVELY BUILT binary and \\\n                 the host journal is honoured by the interpreter only.\\n  \\\n                 Refusing to run: continuing would {}.\\n  \\\n                 Run the program with `axon run`, which implements the journal.\",\n                match var {\n                    \"AXON_REPLAY\" => {\n                        \"perform the effects for real while looking like a replay\"\n                    }\n                    \"AXON_RECORD\" => {\n                        \"record nothing, leaving an empty journal that reads as a \\\n                         run which touched nothing\"\n                    }\n                    // Measured: interp wrote 2 ledger entries for one\n                    // `write_file`; the native binary did not create the ledger\n                    // file at all.\n                    _ => {\n                        \"perform capability-bearing operations while writing NO \\\n                         audit entries — a ledger indistinguishable from a run \\\n                         that did nothing\"\n                    }\n                }\n            );\n            std::process::exit(2);\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// Seed the C RNG that native `random_i64`/`random_f64` lower to.\n///\n/// Native codegen emits a bare `rand()` and NOTHING ever called `srand`, so a\n/// natively built binary produced the SAME \"random\" value on every run, on\n/// every machine, and ignored `AXON_SEED` entirely. Measured on one program:\n///\n/// ```text\n/// interp   seed=1 -> 269761   seed=42 -> 75435    seed=999 -> 243488\n/// native   seed=1 -> 289383   seed=42 -> 289383   seed=999 -> 289383\n/// ```\n///\n/// Two defects in one: a documented control (`AXON_SEED`) silently dropped by\n/// one engine, and — worse, because it is not a parity question — randomness\n/// that is not random. `CLAUDE.md` gives `AXON_CLOCK` the caveat \"Honoured by\n/// interp AND native\"; the `AXON_SEED` row carries no engine caveat at all, so\n/// a reader has no way to know.\n///\n/// Called once from `main`'s prologue, alongside the recursion guard and the\n/// adaptive registry. When `AXON_SEED` is unset the seed comes from the clock,\n/// which restores ordinary randomness; when it is set the run is reproducible,\n/// which is what the variable is for.\n#[no_mangle]\npub extern \"C\" fn __axon_rt_seed_rng() {\n    let seed = match std::env::var(\"AXON_SEED\")\n        .ok()\n        .and_then(|v| v.trim().parse::<u64>().ok())\n    {\n        Some(s) => s,\n        None => std::time::SystemTime::now()\n            .duration_since(std::time::UNIX_EPOCH)\n            .map(|d| d.as_nanos() as u64)\n            .unwrap_or(1),\n    };\n    // SAFETY: `srand` takes an unsigned int and returns nothing.\n    unsafe { libc::srand(seed as libc::c_uint) };\n}\n\n/// Deterministic virtual clock for the NATIVE runtime — the mirror of\n/// `axon-core`'s `clock.rs`. See that module for the full rationale; the short\n/// version is that a monotonic virtual clock (not a frozen one) is what lets a\n/// run that reads the time be replayed without changing what the program\n/// computes.\n///\n/// `AXON_CLOCK=<start_ms>` or `<start_ms>:<tick_ms>`; tick defaults to 1 and may\n/// be 0 for a clock that only moves when `sleep_ms` advances it.\nmod vclock {\n    use std::sync::atomic::{AtomicBool, AtomicI64, Ordering};\n\n    static ENABLED: AtomicBool = AtomicBool::new(false);\n    static INITIALIZED: AtomicBool = AtomicBool::new(false);\n    static CURRENT: AtomicI64 = AtomicI64::new(0);\n    static TICK: AtomicI64 = AtomicI64::new(1);\n\n    fn init_from_env() {\n        if INITIALIZED.swap(true, Ordering::SeqCst) {\n            return;\n        }\n        let Ok(raw) = std::env::var(\"AXON_CLOCK\") else {\n            return;\n        };\n        let raw = raw.trim();\n        if raw.is_empty() {\n            return;\n        }\n        let (start, tick) = match raw.split_once(':') {\n            Some((s, t)) => (s.trim().parse::<i64>(), t.trim().parse::<i64>().ok()),\n            None => (raw.parse::<i64>(), None),\n        };\n        // Malformed input leaves the clock OFF rather than inventing a timeline.\n        if let Ok(start) = start {\n            CURRENT.store(start, Ordering::SeqCst);\n            TICK.store(tick.unwrap_or(1).max(0), Ordering::SeqCst);\n            ENABLED.store(true, Ordering::SeqCst);\n        }\n    }\n\n    fn enabled() -> bool {\n        init_from_env();\n        ENABLED.load(Ordering::SeqCst)\n    }\n\n    /// The virtual \"now\", advancing by `tick`. `None` → use the real clock.\n    pub fn now_ms() -> Option<i64> {\n        if !enabled() {\n            return None;\n        }\n        Some(CURRENT.fetch_add(TICK.load(Ordering::SeqCst), Ordering::SeqCst))\n    }\n\n    /// Advance by `ms`. `false` → the caller should really sleep.\n    pub fn advance(ms: i64) -> bool {\n        if !enabled() {\n            return false;\n        }\n        CURRENT.fetch_add(ms.max(0), Ordering::SeqCst);\n        true\n    }\n}\n\n// ── Phase 10: Do NOT combine `codegen` with `serde-json`.\"\n    );\n    eprintln!(\n        \"hint: use `axon run <file.ax>` — it executes via the interpreter, no codegen needed.\"\n    );\n    process::exit(1);\n}\n\n#[cfg(feature = \"codegen\")]\n#[allow(clippy::too_many_arguments)]\nfn cmd_build(\n    files: Vec<PathBuf>,\n    out: Option<PathBuf>,\n    release: bool,\n    target: Option<String>,\n    no_cache: bool,\n    cache_dir: Option<PathBuf>,\n    freestanding: bool,\n    linker_script: Option<PathBuf>,\n    emit_obj: bool,\n    emit_llvm: bool,\n    host: Option<String>,\n    emit_manifest: bool,\n) {\n    if files.is_empty() {\n        eprintln!(\"error: no source files specified\");\n        process::exit(1);\n    }\n    for f in &files {\n        validate_ax_extension(f);\n    }\n\n    // NATIVE EFFECT-CEILING PARITY: refuse to emit an artifact that would\n    // silently drop an active ambient ceiling.\n    //\n    // `AXON_ALLOWED_EFFECTS` and `AXON_BUDGET_TOKENS` are enforced by the\n    // interpreter's F5 hook and read NOWHERE in `codegen/`. {"parentUuid":"53b4d109-029f-4ea5-a170-712a183fb3a0","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"77a899ff-1e86-45ed-8041-dbe3ce017ec3","agentId":"a8133cd58490ff89f","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_019f6gC2A4gso8FXRA9M8umJ","type":"tool_result","content":"#!/usr/bin/env bash\n# build_outcome_regression.sh — the instrument that makes a backend change's\n# claim CHECKABLE, in both directions.\n#\n# Two measurements, one script:\n#\n#   1. BUILD-OUTCOME CORPUS DIFF. `axon build` every `.ax` program under\n#      `examples/` and `crates/axon-core/tests/fixtures/` and diff the exit\n#      status of each against a committed baseline. The diff is SYMMETRIC:\n#      a program that starts FAILING and a program that starts PASSING are both\n#      regressions to report. A one-directional baseline stops meaning anything\n#      the moment something shifts underneath it — it keeps passing while the\n#      thing it was supposed to pin has moved.\n#\n#   2. INTERPRETER↔NATIVE DIFFERENTIAL on a declared program list. Compares\n#      printed STDOUT, never exit codes: Axon remaps 2..=15 and 101 onto 1, so\n#      an exit-code-only comparison passes on a wrong value (13 real divergences\n#      were found that way once the comparison moved to stdout). The interpreter\n#      is reference semantics (invariant I-2).\n#\n#      A native build that REFUSES (E0910, \"sound by refusal\") is its own\n#      reported outcome — `native-refused-e0910`. It is not a pass (the two\n#      engines were never compared) and not a skip (the refusal is a designed\n#      promise and is worth pinning). Its baseline row is what makes a refusal\n#      that silently BECOMES a miscompile visible.\n#\n# Occasion: a native-backend defect where a non-Unit body that lowers to no\n# value has `const_zero()` synthesized for it. On an aborting build that is a\n# harmless placeholder; on a SUCCEEDING build it is an invented answer. Two\n# fixes are expected (a codegen-error safety net, then the match-arm payload\n# type propagation that makes the construct lower for real), and both of them\n# make claims of the form \"exactly these programs move, and nothing else does\".\n# That claim is only checkable against a baseline that is trusted in BOTH\n# directions, which is what this is.\n#\n# ── What this CANNOT establish ───────────────────────────────────────────────\n#   * A build exit status is not a correct program. 166 programs build clean;\n#     this says only that the same 166 build clean, not that any of them is\n#     right. The fabricated-return class is invisible to the sweep BY\n#     CONSTRUCTION — that is why part 2 exists.\n#   * The stdout differential is blind wherever a fabricated value does not\n#     reach stdout. Measured: `examples/asi/search_rank.ax` is one of the two\n#     programs that reach the fabricating branch on a SUCCEEDING build, and\n#     both engines still print identical stdout (they both stop at the same\n#     `@[verify]` failure in `deploy_gate`). Its `agree` row is a\n#     change-detector, not a certificate.\n#   * Stderr is reported but not compared. The two engines word the same\n#     @[verify] failure differently — a real, pre-existing gap documented in\n#     `exit_code_parity.sh` — so an equality assertion there would fail for a\n#     reason that has nothing to do with this measurement.\n#   * The differential covers a DECLARED list, not all 328 programs. Most of\n#     the corpus needs stdin, the network, or an API key to run.\n#\n# ── Usage ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n#   scripts/build_outcome_regression.sh              # full check (sweep + differential)\n#   scripts/build_outcome_regression.sh --build-only # corpus diff only\n#   scripts/build_outcome_regression.sh --diff-only  # differential only (fast)\n#   scripts/build_outcome_regression.sh --self-test  # prove the comparator FAILS when it should\n#   scripts/build_outcome_regression.sh --record     # rewrite BOTH baselines (deliberate act)\n#   BASELINE=… DIFF_BASELINE=… JOBS=6 AXON=path/to/axon  scripts/build_outcome_regression.sh\n...\n164:# AXON_* vars that change a BUILD outcome or a run's observable output; they are\n166:# baseline. AXON_AI_MOCK is set PER CASE below where a program needs it.\n167:unset AXON_STRICT AXON_RECORD AXON_REPLAY AXON_AI_MOCK AXON_INTENT_GEN \\\n168:      AXON_ALLOWED_EFFECTS AXON_BUDGET_TOKENS AXON_SEED AXON_CLOCK AXON_AI_REPLAY\n350:  [ \"$mock\" = \"1\" ] && env_prefix=(env AXON_AI_MOCK=1)","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"bf6ca0c6-0753-4a27-a0a2-26ada1c175dd","timestamp":"2026-09-20T19:56:18.159Z","toolUseResult":{"stdout":"#!/usr/bin/env bash\n# build_outcome_regression.sh — the instrument that makes a backend change's\n# claim CHECKABLE, in both directions.\n#\n# Two measurements, one script:\n#\n#   1. {"parentUuid":"f73ae4f1-4685-407d-8605-7e010451d57f","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"77a899ff-1e86-45ed-8041-dbe3ce017ec3","agentId":"a8133cd58490ff89f","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01VPdCATJebBhSH9fj573R2a","type":"tool_result","content":"    );\n    assert!(\n        !d.contains(\"\\\"file\\\":\\\"main.ax\\\"\"),\n        \"the diagnostic is labelled with the entry file — this is the original \\\n         defect: {d}\"\n    );\n    assert!(\n        d.contains(&format!(\"\\\"line\\\":{bad_line}\")),\n        \"expected line {bad_line} (the line `deliberately_broken` is on): {d}\"\n    );\n\n    let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dir);\n}\n\n/// THE SAFETY BOUNDARY MUST BE EQUIVALENT ACROSS ENGINES.\n///\n/// For a program P and an active effect ceiling C:\n///\n///     interp(P, C) permits effect E   iff   native(P, C) permits effect E\n///\n/// Byte-identical behaviour is not required; an equivalent BOUNDARY is. This\n/// asserts the boundary, not the mechanism: the native side passes either by\n/// REFUSING TO BUILD or by emitting a binary that enforces the ceiling. Only\n/// \"built fine and performed the effect anyway\" fails.\n///\n/// Measured before the fix, on exactly this program:\n///\n///     AXON_ALLOWED_EFFECTS=Pure axon run  c.ax  -> sandbox violation, exit 8\n///     AXON_ALLOWED_EFFECTS=Pure axon build c.ax -> binary emitted, exit 0\n///     AXON_ALLOWED_EFFECTS=Pure ./cbin          -> \"IO HAPPENED\",   exit 0\n///\n/// The gap is reachable in production: `axon-guest-init` REFUSES TO BOOT\n/// without an MMDS policy, exports the ceiling into the guest, and a natively\n/// built payload ignores it — a policy attested as applied and then unenforced.\n/// Note the direction: the INTERPRETER is the stricter engine, so \"build native\n/// for speed\" silently removes a control.\n#[test]\nfn a_native_build_cannot_silently_drop_an_ambient_effect_ceiling() {\n    let dir = std::env::temp_dir().join(format!(\"axon_ceiling_{}\", std::process::id()));\n    let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dir);\n    std::fs::create_dir_all(&dir).unwrap();\n    let src = dir.join(\"c.ax\");\n    std::fs::write(\n        &src,\n        \"fn main() -> i64 {\\n    println(\\\"IO HAPPENED\\\")\\n    0\\n}\\n\",\n    )\n    .unwrap();\n\n    // PRECONDITION: the interpreter must actually refuse, or this test proves\n    // nothing about parity — it would be comparing two permissive engines.\n    let run = Command::new(env!(\"CARGO_BIN_EXE_axon\"))\n        .args([\"run\", src.to_str().unwrap()])\n        .env(\"AXON_ALLOWED_EFFECTS\", \"Pure\")\n        .output()\n        .expect(\"axon run\");\n    assert_eq!(\n        run.status.code(),\n        Some(8),\n        \"precondition: the interpreter must refuse `println` under a Pure ceiling \\\n         (exit 8). Got {:?}. stderr: {}\",\n        run.status.code(),\n        String::from_utf8_lossy(&run.stderr)\n    );\n\n    // EVERY codegen entry point, not just `axon build`.\n    //\n    // The first version of this test checked `axon build` alone, and\n    // `axon target build --engine codegen --target wasm32` walked past the\n    // refusal and emitted an artifact under an active ceiling (verified,\n    // exit 0). One guarded verb is not a guarded boundary.\n    let wasm_out = Command::new(env!(\"CARGO_BIN_EXE_axon\"))\n        .args([\n            \"target\",\n            \"build\",\n            \"--engine\",\n            \"codegen\",\n            \"--target\",\n            \"wasm32\",\n            src.to_str().unwrap(),\n        ])\n        .current_dir(&dir)\n        .env(\"AXON_ALLOWED_EFFECTS\", \"Pure\")\n        .output()\n        .expect(\"axon target build\");\n    assert!(\n        !wasm_out.status.success(),\n        \"`axon target build --engine codegen` emitted an artifact under an \\\n         active ceiling: {}\",\n        String::from_utf8_lossy(&wasm_out.stdout)\n    );\n\n    let bin = dir.join(\"cbin\");\n    let build = Command::new(env!(\"CARGO_BIN_EXE_axon\"))\n        .args([\"build\", src.to_str().unwrap(), \"-o\", bin.to_str().unwrap()])\n        .env(\"AXON_ALLOWED_EFFECTS\", \"Pure\")\n        .output()\n        .expect(\"axon build\");\n\n    if build.status.success() {\n        // The build chose to emit. Then the ARTIFACT must enforce the ceiling.\n        assert!(bin.exists(), \"build reported success but emitted no binary\");\n        let ran = Command::new(&bin)\n            .env(\"AXON_ALLOWED_EFFECTS\", \"Pure\")\n            .output()\n            .expect(\"run the emitted binary\");\n        let out = String::from_utf8_lossy(&ran.stdout);\n        assert!(\n            !out.contains(\"IO HAPPENED\"),\n            \"ENGINE PARITY VIOLATION: the interpreter refused this program under \\\n             AXON_ALLOWED_EFFECTS=Pure (exit 8), but `axon build` emitted a binary \\\n             that performed the IO anyway and exited {:?}. A native build must \\\n             either refuse to emit, or emit an artifact that enforces the ceiling \\\n             — it must not silently drop the policy.\",\n            ran.status.code()\n        );\n    } else {\n        // The build refused. That is the other legitimate answer; check it\n        // refused for THIS reason and not incidentally (a missing linker, a\n        // parse error, an interp-only build would all exit non-zero too).\n        let err = String::from_utf8_lossy(&build.stderr);\n        assert!(\n            err.contains(\"AXON_ALLOWED_EFFECTS\"),\n            \"the build failed, but not because of the ceiling — a refusal for an \\\n             unrelated reason does not establish parity. stderr: {err}\"\n        );\n        assert!(\n            !bin.exists(),\n            \"the build refused but left a binary behind at {}\",\n            bin.display()\n        );\n    }\n\n    let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dir);\n}\n\n/// `AXON_SEED` must reach the NATIVE engine, and native randomness must be\n/// random when it is unset.\n///\n/// Codegen lowers `random_i64` to a bare C `rand()`, and `srand` was called\n/// nowhere. Measured before the fix, on this program:\n///\n/// ```text\n/// interp   seed=1 -> 269761   seed=42 -> 75435    seed=999 -> 243488\n/// native   seed=1 -> 289383   seed=42 -> 289383   seed=999 -> 289383\n/// ```\n///\n/// Two defects in one. `AXON_SEED` — documented with no engine caveat, unlike\n/// the `AXON_CLOCK` row three lines away which says \"Honoured by interp AND\n/// native\" — was silently dropped by one engine. And, worse because it is not a\n/// parity question, a native binary's randomness was CONSTANT: the same value\n/// on every run and every machine, which matters for anything that reaches for\n/// `random_*` to get a nonce or a sample.\n///\n/// This asserts the CONTRACT, not equality with the interpreter: native and\n/// interp use different generators, so the same seed yields different values.\n/// What must hold is that a seed determines the run, and that an absent seed\n/// does not.\n#[test]\nfn axon_seed_reaches_the_native_engine_and_absent_means_random() {\n    let dir = std::env::temp_dir().join(format!(\"axon_seed_{}\", std::process::id()));\n    let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dir);","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"19f15f93-b761-4e67-9530-1e8c1b7f01f3","timestamp":"2026-09-20T19:56:30.212Z","toolUseResult":{"stdout":"    );\n    assert!(\n        !d.contains(\"\\\"file\\\":\\\"main.ax\\\"\"),\n        \"the diagnostic is labelled with the entry file — this is the original \\\n         defect: {d}\"\n    );\n    assert!(\n        d.contains(&format!(\"\\\"line\\\":{bad_line}\")),\n        \"expected line {bad_line} (the line `deliberately_broken` is on): {d}\"\n    );\n\n    let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dir);\n}\n\n/// THE SAFETY BOUNDARY MUST BE EQUIVALENT ACROSS ENGINES.\n///\n/// For a program P and an active effect ceiling C:\n///\n///     interp(P, C) permits effect E   iff   native(P, C) permits effect E\n///\n/// Byte-identical behaviour is not required; an equivalent BOUNDARY is. Got {:?}. stderr: {}\",\n        run.status.code(),\n        String::from_utf8_lossy(&run.stderr)\n    );\n\n    // EVERY codegen entry point, not just `axon build`.\n    //\n    // The first version of this test checked `axon build` alone, and\n    // `axon target build --engine codegen --target wasm32` walked past the\n    // refusal and emitted an artifact under an active ceiling (verified,\n    // exit 0). One guarded verb is not a guarded boundary.\n    let wasm_out = Command::new(env!(\"CARGO_BIN_EXE_axon\"))\n        .args([\n            \"target\",\n            \"build\",\n            \"--engine\",\n            \"codegen\",\n            \"--target\",\n            \"wasm32\",\n            src.to_str().unwrap(),\n        ])\n        .current_dir(&dir)\n        .env(\"AXON_ALLOWED_EFFECTS\", \"Pure\")\n        .output()\n        .expect(\"axon target build\");\n    assert!(\n        !wasm_out.status.success(),\n        \"`axon target build --engine codegen` emitted an artifact under an \\\n         active ceiling: {}\",\n        String::from_utf8_lossy(&wasm_out.stdout)\n    );\n\n    let bin = dir.join(\"cbin\");\n    let build = Command::new(env!(\"CARGO_BIN_EXE_axon\"))\n        .args([\"build\", src.to_str().unwrap(), \"-o\", bin.to_str().unwrap()])\n        .env(\"AXON_ALLOWED_EFFECTS\", \"Pure\")\n        .output()\n        .expect(\"axon build\");\n\n    if build.status.success() {\n        // The build chose to emit. Then the ARTIFACT must enforce the ceiling.\n        assert!(bin.exists(), \"build reported success but emitted no binary\");\n        let ran = Command::new(&bin)\n            .env(\"AXON_ALLOWED_EFFECTS\", \"Pure\")\n            .output()\n            .expect(\"run the emitted binary\");\n        let out = String::from_utf8_lossy(&ran.stdout);\n        assert!(\n            !out.contains(\"IO HAPPENED\"),\n            \"ENGINE PARITY VIOLATION: the interpreter refused this program under \\\n             AXON_ALLOWED_EFFECTS=Pure (exit 8), but `axon build` emitted a binary \\\n             that performed the IO anyway and exited {:?}. A native build must \\\n             either refuse to emit, or emit an artifact that enforces the ceiling \\\n             — it must not silently drop the policy.\",\n            ran.status.code()\n        );\n    } else {\n        // The build refused. That is the other legitimate answer; check it\n        // refused for THIS reason and not incidentally (a missing linker, a\n        // parse error, an interp-only build would all exit non-zero too).\n        let err = String::from_utf8_lossy(&build.stderr);\n        assert!(\n            err.contains(\"AXON_ALLOWED_EFFECTS\"),\n            \"the build failed, but not because of the ceiling — a refusal for an \\\n             unrelated reason does not establish parity. stderr: {err}\"\n        );\n        assert!(\n            !bin.exists(),\n            \"the build refused but left a binary behind at {}\",\n            bin.display()\n        );\n    }\n\n    let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dir);\n}\n\n/// `AXON_SEED` must reach the NATIVE engine, and native randomness must be\n/// random when it is unset.\n///\n/// Codegen lowers `random_i64` to a bare C `rand()`, and `srand` was called\n/// nowhere. Measured before the fix, on this program:\n///\n/// ```text\n/// interp   seed=1 -> 269761   seed=42 -> 75435    seed=999 -> 243488\n/// native   seed=1 -> 289383   seed=42 -> 289383   seed=999 -> 289383\n/// ```\n///\n/// Two defects in one. `AXON_SEED` — documented with no engine caveat, unlike\n/// the `AXON_CLOCK` row three lines away which says \"Honoured by interp AND\n/// native\" — was silently dropped by one engine. And, worse because it is not a\n/// parity question, a native binary's randomness was CONSTANT: the same value\n/// on every run and every machine, which matters for anything that reaches for\n/// `random_*` to get a nonce or a sample.\n///\n/// This asserts the CONTRACT, not equality with the interpreter: native and\n/// interp use different generators, so the same seed yields different values.\n/// What must hold is that a seed determines the run, and that an absent seed\n/// does not.\n#[test]\nfn axon_seed_reaches_the_native_engine_and_absent_means_random() {\n    let dir = std::env::temp_dir().join(format!(\"axon_seed_{}\", std::process::id()));\n    let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dir);","stderr":"","interrupted":false,"isImage":false,"noOutputExpected":false},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"f73ae4f1-4685-407d-8605-7e010451d57f","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/home/cklaus/projects/axon","sessionId":"b08bcf98-5d80-4faf-ab6a-34e0772b5071","version":"2.1.223","gitBranch":"main","slug":"valiant-dancing-bird"}

The\n# cross-platform `AxonRuntime` lifecycle CORE (`mobile_ios::runtime`) compiles on\n# EVERY host (so its tests run on the Linux gate); only the `#[no_mangle] extern\n# \"C\"` entry points the Swift app calls are additionally `cfg(target_os=\"ios\")`-\n# gated, so Linux never emits them. This feature is OFF by default — the Linux\n# `gate.sh` never sets it, so it cannot regress the Linux build.\nios = []\n\n[dependencies]\n# R13 slice 4: the shared `native::gfx` mock impl (one impl, two engines). Not\n# pulled on wasm/Windows — the guard is a no-op there.\n[target.'cfg(unix)'.dependencies]\nlibc = \"0.2\"\n=== rt ai/provenance/goal ===\ncrates/axon-rt/src/lib.rs:2910:/// (read_file / ai_complete / str_split), not just literals. I-2-canonical.\n=== rt provenance ===\n//! Provenance logger for `@[adaptive]` functions.\n//!\n//! Each invocation of an `@[adaptive]` function appends one JSON line to\n//! `~/.cache/axon/provenance.jsonl` (or `$XDG_CACHE_HOME/axon/...`) recording\n//! the function name, an event tag (`\"call\"` / `\"return\"` / `\"event\"`), an\n//! optional payload string, and a timestamp.  In addition to the on-disk log,\n//! the runtime keeps an in-memory `Mutex<Vec<Record>>` of return-value\n//! observations so `__axon_goal_run` can compute a best observed score\n//! without re-parsing the JSONL file.\n//!\n//! ABI symbols exported here:\n//! * `__axon_provenance_log(fn_name, payload)` — original Layer-1 entry point.\n//!   Used by codegen at adaptive prologue / return sites with `\"call\"` /\n//!   `\"return\"` payloads.  Backward-compatible: nothing about the on-disk\n//!   format has changed.\n//! * `__axon_provenance_log_ret_i64(fn_name, ret)` — Layer-2 addition.  Codegen\n//!   calls this immediately before `build_return` in an `@[adaptive]` function\n//!   whose return type is `i64`.  Records the return value into the in-memory\n//!   log AND appends a JSON line with `\"event\":\"return\",\"score\":<f64>`.\n//! * `__axon_provenance_log_ret_f64(fn_name, ret)` — same, for `f64` returns.\n//!\n//! Reading the in-memory log:\n//! * `provenance_records_for(name)` — Rust-side helper returning a snapshot of\n//!   all records whose `fn_name` matches `name`.  Consumed by\n//!   [`crate::goal::__axon_goal_run`].\n//!\n//! All filesystem errors are silently swallowed — this logger must never panic\n//! in user code.\n\nuse std::fs::{self, OpenOptions};\nuse std::io::Write;\nuse std::path::PathBuf;\nuse std::sync::Mutex;\nuse std::time::{SystemTime, UNIX_EPOCH};\n\n// ── In-memory record store ────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n\n/// A single recorded execution of an `@[adaptive]` function's return site.\n///\n/// Stored in a process-global `Mutex<Vec<Record>>`. The\n// key is sha256(model\\0prompt); the line is `<key> <tokens> <response-hex>`, so a\n// response containing newlines/quotes needs no escaping.\n\n/// The replay-cache file path from `AXON_AI_REPLAY` (None when unset/empty).\npub(super) fn ai_replay_path() -> Option<std::path::PathBuf> {\n    std::env::var(\"AXON_AI_REPLAY\")\n        .ok()\n        .filter(|s| !s.is_empty())\n        .map(std::path::PathBuf::from)\n}\n\nfn ai_replay_key(prompt: &str, model: &str) -> String {\n    sha256_hex(&format!(\"{model}\\u{0}{prompt}\"))\n}\n\nfn hex_encode(bytes: &[u8]) -> String {\n    let mut s = String::with_capacity(bytes.len() * 2);\n    for b in bytes {\n        s.push_str(&format!(\"{b:02x}\"));\n    }\n    s\n}\n\nfn hex_decode(s: &str) -> Option<Vec<u8>> {\n    if !s.len().is_multiple_of(2) {\n        return None;\n    }\n=== AI_REPLAY main.rs ===\ncrates/axon-core/src/clock.rs:5://! `AXON_SEED` makes every `random_*` reproducible and `AXON_AI_REPLAY` makes\ncrates/axon-core/src/main.rs:3860:/// half is the provenance log + AXON_AI_REPLAY).\ncrates/axon-core/src/main.rs:4054:/// Combined with `AXON_AI_REPLAY=<cache>` the user gets a byte-identical replay\ncrates/axon-core/src/interp/builtins.rs:81:            \"{who}: malformed AXON_AI_REPLAY entry {cached:?} (expected \\\"<value>|<confidence>\\\") \\\ncrates/axon-core/src/interp/builtins.rs:5633:                // F2 (ROADMAP §9.5): deterministic REPLAY. The Z3-backed *proof model* (`smt::check_mint_tcb_obligation`) is the\n//! separate, opt-in path that PRODUCES these certs; this gate only CHECKS them.\n//! Default (flag off) behavior is byte-unchanged: `Ok(())` and no output.\n//!\n//! The certified obligations are the in-code mint lowering: the shipped\n//! `examples/proofs/*.obl` are locked equal to `axon_certcheck::{mint_o1, mint_o2}`\n//! by `axon_certcheck::obligations::shipped_obligations_match_the_code_lowering`,\n//! so a hand-edited `.obl` (certifying a formula that is NOT the mint law) is\n//! caught — the certified obligation IS the mint law, by test, not by trust.\n\npub const PINNED_MINT_O1_OBLIGATION: &str = include_str!(\"../../../examples/proofs/mint_o1.obl\");\npub const PINNED_MINT_O1_CERTIFICATE: &str = include_str!(\"../../../examples/proofs/mint_o1.cert\");\npub const PINNED_MINT_OBLIGATION: &str = include_str!(\"../../../examples/proofs/mint_o2.obl\");\npub const PINNED_MINT_CERTIFICATE: &str = include_str!(\"../../../examples/proofs/mint_o2.cert\");\n\n/// Whether the `--require-certificates` policy is active (env `AXON_REQUIRE_CERTS=1`).\npub fn require_certificates_enabled() -> bool {\n    std::env::var(\"AXON_REQUIRE_CERTS\")\n        .map(|v| v == \"1\" || v.eq_ignore_ascii_case(\"true\"))\n        .unwrap_or(false)\n}\n\n/// R23: gate BOTH mint TCB obligations (O1 boolean attenuation + O2 budget carve)\n/// on a solver-free certificate check when `--require-certificates` is on. `Ok(())`\n/// when the policy is off OR both certificates validate; `Err((E1611, …))` (fail\n/// closed) if EITHER obligation is not certificate-discharged. The check uses NO\n/// solver — Z3 is not in the path. Available in EVERY build.\npub fn check_mint_certificate() -> Result<(), (&'static str, String)> {\n    let require = require_certificates_enabled();\n    // BOTH O1 and O2 must be certificate-discharged (fail closed on either).\n    check_one_mint_obligation(\n        \"O1\",\n        PINNED_MINT_O1_OBLIGATION,\n        PINNED_MINT_O1_CERTIFICATE,\n        require,\n    )?;\n    check_one_mint_obligation(\n        \"O2\",\n        PINNED_MINT_OBLIGATION,\n        PINNED_MINT_CERTIFICATE,\n        require,\n    )?;\n    Ok(())\n}\n\n/// Run the cert gate in the run/build path: fail closed on a bad cert, and emit\n/// the legibility line when the policy is active. Always compiled.\npub fn enforce_or_exit() {\n    if let Err((code, msg, exit)) = enforcement_decision() {\n        eprintln!(\"{code}: {msg}\");\n        std::process::exit(exit);\n=== cert_gate callers ===\ncrates/axon-core/src/smt.rs:2526:        use crate::cert_gate::{\ncrates/axon-core/src/main.rs:3518:    axon_core::cert_gate::enforce_or_exit();\ncrates/axon-core/src/main.rs:4786:    axon_core::cert_gate::enforce_or_exit();\ncrates/axon-core/src/main.rs:5565:    axon_core::cert_gate::enforce_or_exit();\ncrates/axon-core/src/main.rs:5675:    // R23 cert gate now runs in EVERY build (see `cert_gate::enforce_or_exit`\ncrates/axon-core/src/main.rs:5988:    axon_core::cert_gate::enforce_or_exit();\ncrates/axon-core/src/main.rs:6892:    axon_core::cert_gate::enforce_or_exit();\ncrates/axon-core/src/main.rs:7953:    axon_core::cert_gate::enforce_or_exit();","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"a6a88d77-2124-4e6e-94b1-419bdb02a81d","timestamp":"2026-09-20T19:57:07.291Z","toolUseResult":{"stdout":"=== goal.rs rt ===\n=== cert_gate ===\n//! linked. {"parentUuid":"33629872-a9c2-4140-8ddf-4452e8d2fc6c","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"77a899ff-1e86-45ed-8041-dbe3ce017ec3","agentId":"a8133cd58490ff89f","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_019V2UE1bbFWvzM69RNXRxbS","type":"tool_result","content":"    //    then skipped the whole policy block — no effect ceiling, no token cap,\n    //    no seccomp — and exec'd the guest anyway. The sandbox this binary\n    //    exists to install was simply absent, and the only evidence was a line\n    //    saying \"running without policy\" among the boot messages.\n    //\n    //    The soft-fail was justified as \"running outside axon-vm\". Nothing in\n    //    the workspace runs it that way: it is `/sbin/init` in\n    //    `axon-rootfs.ext4`, PID 1 of a microVM, and no script or test invokes\n    //    it directly. So the case being preserved had no caller, while the case\n    //    being broken — a VM that boots but cannot reach MMDS — is exactly when\n    //    a capability policy matters.\n    //\n    //    The developer case is still reachable, but must now be ASKED for.\n    let allow_unpoliced = env::var(\"AXON_GUEST_ALLOW_NO_POLICY\")\n        .map(|v| v == \"1\" || v.eq_ignore_ascii_case(\"true\"))\n        .unwrap_or(false);\n    let policy = match read_mmds() {\n        Ok(p) => p,\n        Err(e) => {\n            eprintln!(\"[axon-guest-init] MMDS unavailable ({e})\");\n            None\n        }\n    };\n    match policy_decision(policy.is_some(), allow_unpoliced) {\n        PolicyDecision::Apply | PolicyDecision::ProceedUnpoliced => {}\n        PolicyDecision::Refuse => {\n            eprintln!(\n                \"[axon-guest-init] REFUSING to start the guest: no capability policy was \\\n                 loaded, so there would be no effect ceiling, no token cap and no seccomp \\\n                 filter. Set AXON_GUEST_ALLOW_NO_POLICY=1 to run unpoliced on purpose \\\n                 (development only).\"\n            );\n            process::exit(1);\n        }\n    }\n    if policy.is_none() {\n        eprintln!(\n            \"[axon-guest-init] WARNING: AXON_GUEST_ALLOW_NO_POLICY is set — the guest is \\\n             running with NO effect ceiling, NO token cap and NO seccomp filter.\"\n        );\n    }\n\n    // 3. Fork.\n    let child_pid = unsafe { libc::fork() };\n    match child_pid {\n        -1 => {\n            eprintln!(\n                \"[axon-guest-init] fork failed: {}\",\n                std::io::Error::last_os_error()\n            );\n            process::exit(1);\n        }\n        0 => child_main(policy, &binary, &exec_args),\n        pid => supervisor_main(pid),\n    }\n}\n\n/// What to do about the policy we did (or did not) load.\n///\n/// A value rather than inline control flow so it can be tested: this crate is a\n/// PID-1 binary whose main path ends in `exec`, and it had no tests at all — so\n/// the rule that decides whether an unpoliced guest may start was expressed\n/// only in code that cannot be run from a test.\n#[derive(Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]\nenum PolicyDecision {\n    /// A policy was loaded; apply it.\n    Apply,\n    /// No policy, and the operator explicitly allowed running without one.\n    ProceedUnpoliced,\n    /// No policy and no explicit override — do not start the guest.\n    Refuse,\n}\n\nfn policy_decision(have_policy: bool, allow_unpoliced: bool) -> PolicyDecision {\n    match (have_policy, allow_unpoliced) {\n        (true, _) => PolicyDecision::Apply,\n        (false, true) => PolicyDecision::ProceedUnpoliced,\n        // Fail closed: an absent policy is not a permissive one.\n        (false, false) => PolicyDecision::Refuse,\n    }\n}\n\n// ── Child: apply policy then exec ─────────────────────────────────────────────\n\nfn child_main(policy: Option<MmdsPayload>, binary: &str, exec_args: &[String]) -> ! {\n    if let Some(p) = &policy {\n        // Export identity to the Axon runtime (provenance log, ai_complete tier).\n        if let Some(principal) = &p.principal {\n            env::set_var(\"AXON_PRINCIPAL\", principal);\n        }\n        if let Some(tokens) = p.budget_tokens {\n            env::set_var(\"AXON_BUDGET_TOKENS\", tokens.to_string());\n        }\n        if let Some(run_id) = &p.run_id {\n            env::set_var(\"AXON_RUN_ID\", run_id);\n        }\n        if let Some(effects) = &p.allowed_effects {\n            env::set_var(\"AXON_ALLOWED_EFFECTS\", effects.join(\",\"));\n        }\n        if let Some(hash) = &p.source_hash {\n            env::set_var(\"AXON_SOURCE_HASH\", hash);\n        }\n\n        // Apply seccomp AFTER setting env vars (set_var would be blocked after).\n        if let Some(bpf_b64) = &p.seccomp_bpf_b64 {\n            if let Err(e) = apply_seccomp(bpf_b64) {\n                eprintln!(\"[axon-guest-init] seccomp apply failed: {e}\");\n                process::exit(1);\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    exec_process(binary, exec_args)\n}\n\n// ── Entropy re-seed ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n\nfn reseed_entropy() {\n    // The VM may have restored from a snapshot (repeating the RNG pool).\n    // Reading from /dev/urandom triggers mixing of the virtio-rng hardware\n    // entropy source into the kernel pool.","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"43d8e60c-45e1-46eb-b2e8-057c4441a853","timestamp":"2026-09-20T19:57:48.710Z","toolUseResult":{"stdout":"    //    then skipped the whole policy block — no effect ceiling, no token cap,\n    //    no seccomp — and exec'd the guest anyway. Set AXON_GUEST_ALLOW_NO_POLICY=1 to run unpoliced on purpose \\\n                 (development only).\"\n            );\n            process::exit(1);\n        }\n    }\n    if policy.is_none() {\n        eprintln!(\n            \"[axon-guest-init] WARNING: AXON_GUEST_ALLOW_NO_POLICY is set — the guest is \\\n             running with NO effect ceiling, NO token cap and NO seccomp filter.\"\n        );\n    }\n\n    // 3. Fork.\n    let child_pid = unsafe { libc::fork() };\n    match child_pid {\n        -1 => {\n            eprintln!(\n                \"[axon-guest-init] fork failed: {}\",\n                std::io::Error::last_os_error()\n            );\n            process::exit(1);\n        }\n        0 => child_main(policy, &binary, &exec_args),\n        pid => supervisor_main(pid),\n    }\n}\n\n/// What to do about the policy we did (or did not) load.\n///\n/// A value rather than inline control flow so it can be tested: this crate is a\n/// PID-1 binary whose main path ends in `exec`, and it had no tests at all — so\n/// the rule that decides whether an unpoliced guest may start was expressed\n/// only in code that cannot be run from a test.\n#[derive(Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]\nenum PolicyDecision {\n    /// A policy was loaded; apply it.\n    Apply,\n    /// No policy, and the operator explicitly allowed running without one.\n    ProceedUnpoliced,\n    /// No policy and no explicit override — do not start the guest.\n    Refuse,\n}\n\nfn policy_decision(have_policy: bool, allow_unpoliced: bool) -> PolicyDecision {\n    match (have_policy, allow_unpoliced) {\n        (true, _) => PolicyDecision::Apply,\n        (false, true) => PolicyDecision::ProceedUnpoliced,\n        // Fail closed: an absent policy is not a permissive one.\n        (false, false) => PolicyDecision::Refuse,\n    }\n}\n\n// ── Child: apply policy then exec ─────────────────────────────────────────────\n\nfn child_main(policy: Option<MmdsPayload>, binary: &str, exec_args: &[String]) -> ! {\n    if let Some(p) = &policy {\n        // Export identity to the Axon runtime (provenance log, ai_complete tier).\n        if let Some(principal) = &p.principal {\n            env::set_var(\"AXON_PRINCIPAL\", principal);\n        }\n        if let Some(tokens) = p.budget_tokens {\n            env::set_var(\"AXON_BUDGET_TOKENS\", tokens.to_string());\n        }\n        if let Some(run_id) = &p.run_id {\n            env::set_var(\"AXON_RUN_ID\", run_id);\n        }\n        if let Some(effects) = &p.allowed_effects {\n            env::set_var(\"AXON_ALLOWED_EFFECTS\", effects.join(\",\"));\n        }\n        if let Some(hash) = &p.source_hash {\n            env::set_var(\"AXON_SOURCE_HASH\", hash);\n        }\n\n        // Apply seccomp AFTER setting env vars (set_var would be blocked after).\n        if let Some(bpf_b64) = &p.seccomp_bpf_b64 {\n            if let Err(e) = apply_seccomp(bpf_b64) {\n                eprintln!(\"[axon-guest-init] seccomp apply failed: {e}\");\n                process::exit(1);\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    exec_process(binary, exec_args)\n}\n\n// ── Entropy re-seed ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n\nfn reseed_entropy() {\n    // The VM may have restored from a snapshot (repeating the RNG pool).\n    // Reading from /dev/urandom triggers mixing of the virtio-rng hardware\n    // entropy source into the kernel pool.","stderr":"","interrupted":false,"isImage":false,"noOutputExpected":false},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"33629872-a9c2-4140-8ddf-4452e8d2fc6c","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/home/cklaus/projects/axon","sessionId":"b08bcf98-5d80-4faf-ab6a-34e0772b5071","version":"2.1.223","gitBranch":"main","slug":"valiant-dancing-bird"}

Native: fixed OS stack, graceful exit 101 via the signal guard, ceiling not raisable | `cli_run.rs` (interp), `axon-os/tests/acceptance.rs` | N-A — the divergence is *stated in the diagnostic the user sees* |\n\nThe wasm seed gap is the same class as the native one just fixed: both `emit_rng_seed_init` and `emit_interp_only_env_refusal_init` bail on `target_is_wasm` (`codegen/asi.rs:1001` and `:1041`), so a `wasm32-wasip1` artifact — which *does* have an environment — neither honours `AXON_SEED` nor refuses `AXON_REPLAY`/`AXON_RECORD`/`AXON_AUDIT_LEDGER`.\n\n---\n\n## Priority 4 — filesystem / network / process\n\n| name | intended | actual | test | parity |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| `AXON_PATH` | module search path for `mod`/`use` | `lib.rs:497, 908` — shared front-end, so `run`, `check`, `build` all use it | `integration_fixtures.rs`, `all_examples_parity.sh` | CONSISTENT (compile-time) |\n| `AXON_STRICT` | promote E0302 to error;
